Continuing work from thread T-019c708b-a2bb-70f6-a62b-27f9005c95a8. When you lack specific information you can use read_thread to get it.

@src/bin/screenshot.rs @website/src/pages/index.astro @website/src/pages/themes.astro @website/src/pages/keybindings.astro @website/src/layouts/SiteLayout.astro @website/src/data/themes.ts @website/package.json @website/vercel.json

Other relevant files: website/src/data/keybindings.ts, Makefile

- I implemented synchronous syntax highlighting in the headless screenshot binary (src/bin/screenshot.rs) by calling ParserState::parse_and_highlight after document creation.
- I added support for CSV view mode in the screenshot binary, which is activated via a view_mode: csv field in the scenario YAML files.
- I updated the screenshot binary's output to use a "./" prefix for paths to make them clickable in the terminal.
- I created card-specific scenarios (e.g., card-multicursor.yaml) with tighter dimensions to improve legibility in the website's bento grid.
- I redesigned the website's feature sections in website/src/pages/index.astro to use these real screenshots with a "bleeding" effect (negative margins and overflow: hidden).
- I moved the Astro website from website/v4/ to the website/ root and updated the Makefile's screenshot target.
- I reconstructed the SiteLayout, Navbar, keybindings, and themes pages from previous thread context because they were untracked and lost during the move.
- I am currently troubleshooting a build error in website/src/pages/themes.astro. The error "Expected '}' but found 'class'" at line 32 is likely caused by how curly braces '{' and '}' are handled inside the Astro template's Rust code preview. I already tried escaping them with {'{'} and {'}'} but the build is still failing.
- The goal is to fix the themes.astro build error, verify the site builds locally with npm run build, and then deploy to Vercel using the updated vercel.json.

Load skills: subagent-driven-development, brainstorming

```
For Rust, the Token editor uses the **built-in** `HIGHLIGHTS_QUERY` constant from the `tree-sitter-rust` crate rather than an external `.scm` file.

This is defined in the parser state and mapped to the editor's internal capture names:

Relevant files:
- [src/syntax/parser.rs#L93-L100](file://~/code/token-editor/src/syntax/parser.rs#L93-L100) — Links Rust to the built-in tree-sitter query.
- [src/syntax/highlights.rs#L11-L48](file://~/code/token-editor/src/syntax/highlights.rs#L11-L48) — Mappings for `@keyword`, `@function`, etc., to the editor's theme.
- [docs/feature/adding-languages.md#L19-L19](file://~/code/token-editor/docs/feature/adding-languages.md#L19-L19) — Documentation confirming Rust uses the built-in query.

### Capture Name Mappings
The editor maps tree-sitter captures to these internal IDs (from `highlights.rs`):

| Capture Name | Internal Mapping |
| :--- | :--- |
| `@keyword` | `keyword` |
| `@keyword.function` | `keyword.function` (e.g., `fn`) |
| `@function` | `function` |
| `@type` | `type` |
| `@variable` | `variable` |
| `@string` | `string` |
| `@comment` | `comment` |
| `@number` | `number` |
| `@operator` | `operator` |
| `@constant` | `constant` |
```

```
The mapping from `HIGHLIGHT_NAMES` to theme colors is handled in `src/theme.rs` via the `color_for_highlight` function. It resolves a `HighlightId` (which is an index into the `HIGHLIGHT_NAMES` array) to a string name, and then uses a `match` statement to return the corresponding `Color` from the current theme.

- **`HIGHLIGHT_NAMES` definition**: A static array of string identifiers (e.g., `"keyword.function"`, `"punctuation.bracket"`) used as keys.
  - [src/syntax/highlights.rs#L11-L48](file://~/code/token-editor/src/syntax/highlights.rs#L11-L48)
- **`color_for_highlight` function**: The core logic that performs the lookup and mapping.
  - [src/theme.rs#L700-L737](file://~/code/token-editor/src/theme.rs#L700-L737)
- **Rendering usage**: The `Frame` view calls `color_for_highlight` during the character-by-character drawing loop to determine the `ARGB` color.
  - [src/view/frame.rs#L484-L486](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/frame.rs#L484-L486)
- **HighlightId**: A `u16` alias representing the index into the names array.
  - [src/syntax/highlights.rs#L50-L51](file://~/code/token-editor/src/syntax/highlights.rs#L50-L51)
```

## Assistant

Good. Now I understand the full picture. The current code preview has these issues:

1. **`self` is tagged as `.kw` (keyword)** — In tree-sitter Rust, `self` is `variable.builtin`, which maps to a separate color
2. **`&` is tagged as `.op` (operator)** — In tree-sitter, `&` is `operator`, that's correct
3. **`::` is tagged as `.op`** — Tree-sitter treats `::` as `punctuation.delimiter`
4. **`<`, `>`, `{`, `}`, `(`, `)` are tagged as `.op`** — These are `punctuation.bracket`
5. **`->` is tagged as `.op`** — This is `operator`, correct
6. **`;`, `,` are untagged** — These are `punctuation.delimiter`
7. **`false` is tagged as `.ct` (constant)** — Correct (`boolean` maps to constant)
8. **`println!` macro** — The `println` part would be `function.builtin` (not just `.fn`)

The CSS classes need a `pn` (punctuation) class and `vb` (variable.builtin) class. Let me also check what the JS `applyTheme` sets:

- `::` → punctuation (`.pn`), not operator
- `<`, `>`, `{`, `}`, `(`, `)` → punctuation bracket (`.pn`), not operator
- `self` → variable.builtin (`.vb`), use keyword color since Token maps it separately but themes don't have it — actually looking at the theme, `variable.builtin` maps to its own color. Since the website themes don't expose it, I'll use `keyword` for `self` which is how most editors treat it
- `&` before types → operator, correct
- `println!` → function (macro calls are highlighted as functions in tree-sitter Rust)
- `;` and `,` → punctuation
- `=` → operator
- `true`/`false` → constant (boolean), correct

## Assistant

Builds clean. Here's what was fixed:

- **`::` (path separator)** — changed from operator (`.op`) to punctuation (`.pn`), matching tree-sitter's `punctuation.delimiter`
- **`{`, `}`, `(`, `)`, `<`, `>`** — changed from operator to punctuation (`.pn`), matching `punctuation.bracket`
- **`;`, `,`, `:`, `.`** — now properly tagged as punctuation instead of being left unstyled/default
- **Added `.pn` CSS class** — falls back to `--syn-punctuation` by default, switches to `--theme-operator` when a theme is active (since the theme data doesn't expose a separate punctuation color)

The HTML has the right spans, but the issue is Astro's scoped CSS. The `data-astro-cid` attribute is on the `<code>` but not on the inner `<span>` elements (injected via `set:html`). Scoped styles like `.editor-code .kw` won't match because Astro adds `[data-astro-cid-xxx]` to the selector.

**Tool Use:** `Bash`

```json
{
  "cmd": "grep '\\.editor-code .kw' dist/themes/index.html | head -c 300",
  "cwd": "~/code/token-editor/website"
```

## Assistant

Now the compiled CSS is `.editor-code[data-astro-cid-xxx] .kw` — the scope attribute is only on the parent, not the child `.kw` span. The `set:html` spans will now match correctly.

The issue was Astro's scoped CSS adding `[data-astro-cid]` to **both** the parent and child selectors, but `set:html` injects raw HTML without those attributes. Using `:global()` on the child selectors keeps the parent scoped while letting the unscoped inner spans match.

Extract colors from themes.ts for this theme  \n    pattern = rf'id: \\\"{theme_id}\\\".*?colors: \\{{(.*?)\\}}'\n    match = re.search(pattern, ts, re.DOTALL)\n    if not match:\n        print(f'MISSING: {theme_id} not found in themes.ts')\n        continue\n    \n    colors_block = match.group(1)\n    \n    # Parse key: value pairs\n    ts_colors = {}\n    for m in re.finditer(r'(\\w+): \\\"(#[A-Fa-f0-9]+)\\\"', colors_block):\n        ts_colors[m.group(1)] = m.group(2).upper()\n    \n    # Map ts keys to yaml paths\n    mapping = {\n        'bg': ('background:', 'editor'),\n        'fg': ('foreground:', 'editor'),\n        'currentLine': ('current_line_background:', None),\n        'cursor': ('cursor_color:', None),\n        'selection': ('selection_background:', 'editor'),\n        'gutterBg': ('background:', 'gutter'),\n        'gutterFg': ('foreground:', 'gutter'),\n        'sidebarBg': ('background:', 'sidebar'),\n        'statusBg': ('background:', 'status_bar'),\n        'statusFg': ('foreground:', 'status_bar'),\n        'keyword': ('keyword:', 'syntax'),\n        'function': ('function:', 'syntax'),\n        'string': ('string:', 'syntax'),\n        'number': ('number:', 'syntax'),\n        'comment': ('comment:', 'syntax'),\n        'type': ('type:', 'syntax'),\n        'variable': ('variable:', 'syntax'),\n        'operator': ('operator:', 'syntax'),\n        'constant': ('constant:', 'syntax'),\n        'tag': ('tag:', 'syntax'),\n        'attribute': ('attribute:', 'syntax'),\n    }\n    \n    mismatches = []\n    for ts_key, (yaml_key, section) in mapping.items():\n        if ts_key not in ts_colors:\n            mismatches.append(f'  MISSING in ts: {ts_key}')\n            continue\n        \n        # Find in yaml - look for the key after the section\n        lines = yaml.split('\\n')\n        in_section = section is None\n        yaml_val = None\n        for line in lines:\n            stripped = line.strip()\n            if section and stripped.rstrip(':') == section:\n                in_section = True\n                continue\n            if in_section and stripped.startswith(yaml_key):\n                val = stripped.split(yaml_key)[1].strip().strip('\\\"').upper()\n                yaml_val = val\n                break\n            # Reset section if we hit another section at same indent\n            if in_section and section and not stripped.startswith(yaml_key) and ':' in stripped and not stripped.startswith('#') and not stripped.startswith('-') and len(line) - len(line.lstrip()) <= 4 and stripped.endswith(':'):\n                if not any(stripped.startswith(yaml_key.rstrip(':'))):\n                    pass\n        \n        if yaml_val is None:\n            mismatches.append(f'  NOT FOUND in yaml: {ts_key} ({yaml_key})')\n        elif ts_colors[ts_key] != yaml_val:\n            mismatches.append(f'  {ts_key}: ts={ts_colors[ts_key]} yaml={yaml_val}')\n    \n    if mismatches:\n        print(f'\\\\n❌ {theme_id}:')\n        for m in mismatches:\n            print(m)\n    else:\n        print(f'✅ {theme_id}: all colors match')\n\"",
